### Question 49: Gastric Neoplasia: Extranodal Marginal Zone B-Cell Lymphoma (MALToma), H. pylori, and t(11;18)
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The link between chronic *Helicobacter pylori* infection, gastric MALT lymphoma (low-grade extranodal marginal zone B-cell lymphoma), the absence of the t(11;18) translocation, and the efficacy of *H. pylori* eradication as first-line therapy.

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old man presents with dull epigastric pain and early satiety. Upper endoscopy reveals an irregular, thickened mucosal plaque in the gastric body. Biopsy reveals dense infiltration of the lamina propria by small monoclonal B-lymphocytes (CD20+) that form lymphoepithelial lesions by invading and destroying gastric foveolar glands. Rapid urease testing is positive for *Helicobacter pylori*. Cytogenetic analysis is negative for the t(11;18)(q21;q21) chromosomal translocation. The patient is diagnosed with low-grade extranodal marginal zone B-cell lymphoma (MALT lymphoma). What is the definitive first-line therapeutic intervention for this neoplasm, and why does it work?

(A) Eradication of *Helicobacter pylori* with antibiotic quadruple therapy; low-grade MALTomas lack the t(11;18) translocation and are dependent on chronic antigen-driven T-cell stimulation elicited by *H. pylori*; eradicating the bacterium eliminates the immune stimulus, resulting in complete, durable lymphoma remission in >75-80% of patients without chemotherapy or radiation.
(B) Immediate total gastrectomy with splenectomy and Roux-en-Y reconstruction.
(C) High-dose systemic doxorubicin, bleomycin, vinblastine, and dacarbazine (ABVD).
(D) High-dose oral omeprazole monotherapy for life.
(E) Autologous bone marrow transplantation within 24 hours.

#### Correct Answer: A

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Physiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 52-year-old man with epigastric pain and early satiety suggests a gastric pathology, potentially neoplastic or inflammatory.
- **Endoscopy:** Irregular, thickened mucosal plaque in the gastric body points towards a localized lesion, possibly neoplastic or inflammatory.
- **Biopsy:** Dense infiltration of lamina propria by monoclonal B-lymphocytes (CD20+) forming lymphoepithelial lesions (invasion of glands) is characteristic of MALT lymphoma.
- **Rapid Urease Test:** Positive for *H. pylori* establishes a known association with gastric MALT lymphoma.
- **Cytogenetics:** Negative for t(11;18) translocation is crucial. This translocation is characteristic of gastric diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL), a higher-grade lymphoma often associated with chronic inflammation but distinct from *H. pylori*-associated MALT lymphoma.
- **Diagnosis:** Low-grade extranodal marginal zone B-cell lymphoma (MALT lymphoma) confirmed.
- **Question:** Asks for the definitive first-line therapy and its mechanism.

##### 2. Detailed Physiological Mechanism & Molecular Biophysics (消化道分泌生理与上皮转运机制推导)
- **Gastric MALT Lymphoma Pathogenesis:** MALT lymphoma is a low-grade B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma arising in extranodal sites, most commonly the stomach. The pathogenesis of gastric MALT lymphoma is strongly linked to chronic inflammation, particularly that induced by *H. pylori* infection.
- ***H. pylori* and Immune Response:** Chronic *H. pylori* infection triggers a persistent inflammatory response in the gastric mucosa. This involves recruitment of T-cells, B-cells, and plasma cells. The chronic antigen stimulation by *H. pylori* leads to the activation and proliferation of marginal zone B-cells.
- **Role of t(11;18) Translocation:** In most cases of gastric MALT lymphoma associated with *H. pylori* infection, the t(11;18)(q21;q21) chromosomal translocation is absent. This translocation fuses the *API2* gene (encoding an inhibitor of apoptosis) on chromosome 11 with the *MALT1* gene (encoding a kinase involved in B-cell signaling) on chromosome 18. This translocation is characteristic of gastric DLBCL, a higher-grade lymphoma.
- ***H. pylori*-Dependent MALT Lymphoma:** In the absence of the t(11;18) translocation, the MALT lymphoma is considered dependent on the chronic antigenic stimulation provided by *H. pylori*. The persistent inflammation driven by the bacterium leads to the sustained activation of B-cells, eventually resulting in malignant transformation.
- **Therapeutic Principle:** Therefore, the logical first-line treatment strategy for *H. pylori*-positive, t(11;18)-negative gastric MALT lymphoma is to eliminate the underlying cause of chronic inflammation – the *H. pylori* infection itself.
- **Eradication Therapy:** Eradication of *H. pylori* using antibiotic therapy (typically quadruple therapy: proton pump inhibitor, bismuth, metronidazole, and tetracycline) removes the antigenic stimulus. This allows the immune system to regain control, leading to apoptosis of the malignant B-cells and regression of the lymphoma in a significant majority of patients (>75-80%).
- **Alternative Therapies:** Chemotherapy (like ABVD), radiation, or surgery (gastrectomy) are typically reserved for patients who fail *H. pylori* eradication, have refractory disease, or have high-grade lymphoma (often associated with t(11;18)).

##### 3. Step-by-Step Clinical Deduction (临床逻辑逐步推理)
1. The patient has symptoms suggestive of gastric pathology (epigastric pain, early satiety).
2. Endoscopy and biopsy confirm gastric MALT lymphoma (monoclonal B-cells, lymphoepithelial lesions).
3. The patient is positive for *H. pylori*.
4. Cytogenetics show the absence of the t(11;18) translocation.
5. This constellation of findings (gastric MALT lymphoma, *H. pylori*+, t(11;18)-) points to a low-grade lymphoma dependent on chronic *H. pylori* infection.
6. The standard first-line treatment for this specific type of gastric MALT lymphoma is eradication of the causative agent, *H. pylori*.
7. Option A describes *H. pylori* eradication and correctly explains the rationale based on the pathophysiology of *H. pylori*-associated MALT lymphoma lacking the t(11;18) translocation.

##### 4. Distractor Analysis (干扰项逐一深度剖析与鉴别)
- **(B) Immediate total gastrectomy with splenectomy and Roux-en-Y reconstruction:** Gastrectomy is a radical surgical procedure reserved for advanced, refractory, or high-grade gastric lymphomas, or gastric cancer. It is not the first-line treatment for low-grade *H. pylori*-associated MALT lymphoma, which is often curable with antibiotics. Splenectomy is not indicated.
- **(C) High-dose systemic doxorubicin, bleomycin, vinblastine, and dacarbazine (ABVD):** ABVD is a chemotherapy regimen used for Hodgkin lymphoma and some aggressive non-Hodgkin lymphomas. It is not the first-line treatment for low-grade gastric MALT lymphoma, which is highly responsive to *H. pylori* eradication. Chemotherapy is reserved for cases refractory to eradication or for high-grade lymphomas.
- **(D) High-dose oral omeprazole monotherapy for life:** Omeprazole is a proton pump inhibitor used to suppress gastric acid secretion and is part of the *H. pylori* eradication regimen. However, monotherapy is insufficient to eradicate *H. pylori* and is not a treatment for lymphoma itself. While PPIs are used *during* eradication therapy, they are not the definitive treatment for the lymphoma.
- **(E) Autologous bone marrow transplantation within 24 hours:** Bone marrow transplantation is a highly intensive procedure used for certain hematologic malignancies, particularly those refractory to standard therapies or requiring high-dose chemotherapy. It is not indicated as a first-line treatment for low-grade gastric MALT lymphoma.

##### 5. High-Yield Summary Table (高分必备核心对比表)

| Feature                     | Low-Grade Gastric MALT Lymphoma (*H. pylori*+) | High-Grade Gastric DLBCL (often *H. pylori*+) |
| :-------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------------- |
| *H. pylori* Association     | Strong, causative                             | Often present, but not always causative        |
| t(11;18) Translocation      | Absent                                         | Present in ~70-80%                             |
| First-Line Therapy          | *H. pylori* Eradication                         | Chemotherapy (e.g., R-CHOP)                     |
| Response to Eradication     | High (>75-80%)                                 | Low                                           |
| Prognosis                   | Excellent if eradicated                         | Variable, depends on stage and response to chemo |

#### Educational Objective / 核心考点总结:
The key takeaway is the strong association between chronic *H. pylori* infection and low-grade gastric MALT lymphoma, particularly in the absence of the t(11;18) translocation. This understanding dictates that *H. pylori* eradication is the definitive first-line therapy for this specific subtype of gastric lymphoma, offering a high chance of cure without the need for more aggressive treatments like chemotherapy or surgery.

---

### Question 50: Gastric Oncology: Intestinal Type (Glandular, Environmental) vs Diffuse Type (Linitis Plastica, CDH1 / E-Cadherin)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiating the molecular pathogenesis, histology, clinical presentation, and risk factors of intestinal-type versus diffuse-type gastric adenocarcinoma. (区分肠型胃腺癌与弥漫型胃腺癌的分子发病机制、组织学、临床表现和危险因素)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 48-year-old woman presents with persistent early satiety, nausea, and a 12-kg weight loss over the past 6 months. She has no significant past medical history and denies smoking or family history of gastric cancer. Contrast upper GI series reveals a rigid, non-distensible stomach with a thickened, leathery wall resembling a leather bottle ('linitis plastica'). Endoscopic biopsies demonstrate diffuse infiltration of the gastric wall by discohesive, malignant epithelial cells with large intracytoplasmic mucin vacuoles that displace the hyperchromatic nuclei to the periphery ('signet ring cells'). Molecular analysis demonstrates a germline loss-of-function mutation in the CDH1 gene. Which of the following statements correctly differentiates diffuse-type gastric adenocarcinoma from intestinal-type gastric adenocarcinoma?

(A) Diffuse-type gastric cancer is composed of discohesive signet ring cells that infiltrate the gastric wall diffusely with extensive desmoplastic stromal reaction (linitis plastica), driven by loss of E-cadherin (CDH1 mutation), and is unrelated to H. pylori or intestinal metaplasia; Intestinal-type forms cohesive glandular structures, is strongly linked to H. pylori, chronic atrophic gastritis, smoking, and dietary nitrosamines, and commonly presents as an exophytic or ulcerated mass
(B) Diffuse-type gastric cancer arises exclusively from antral G cells
(C) Intestinal-type cancer is caused by mutations in the dystrophin gene
(D) Diffuse-type cancer carries a 100% 5-year cure rate with oral antacids
(E) Intestinal-type cancer occurs exclusively in children under 5 years of age

#### Correct Answer: A

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Physiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 48-year-old woman with early satiety, nausea, weight loss (suggestive of gastric outlet obstruction or advanced malignancy).
- **Imaging:** Upper GI series shows 'linitis plastica' (leather bottle stomach) - diffuse thickening and rigidity of the gastric wall. This is a classic presentation of diffuse-type gastric cancer.
- **Endoscopy/Biopsy:** Diffuse infiltration, discohesive cells, signet ring cells (large mucin vacuoles pushing nuclei to the periphery). This morphology is characteristic of diffuse-type gastric adenocarcinoma.
- **Molecular Genetics:** Germline CDH1 mutation. CDH1 encodes E-cadherin, a cell adhesion molecule. Loss of E-cadherin function leads to decreased cell-cell adhesion, allowing cells to become discohesive and infiltrate tissues diffusely, characteristic of diffuse-type gastric cancer.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the correct statement differentiating diffuse-type from intestinal-type gastric adenocarcinoma.

##### 2. Detailed Physiological Mechanism & Molecular Biophysics (消化道分泌生理与上皮转运机制推导)
- **Gastric Carcinogenesis:** Two main histological types: Intestinal-type and Diffuse-type.
    - **Intestinal-type:** Accounts for ~50% of gastric cancers. Histologically resembles intestinal glands, often forms cohesive masses or ulcers. Arises through a sequence of chronic gastritis -> atrophy -> intestinal metaplasia -> dysplasia -> carcinoma (Correa cascade). Strongly associated with environmental factors like *H. pylori* infection (leading to chronic inflammation and metaplasia), smoking, high salt intake, and dietary nitrosamines. Genetic alterations often involve TP53, APC, KRAS, and microsatellite instability (MSI).
    - **Diffuse-type:** Accounts for ~40-50% of gastric cancers. Characterized by poorly differentiated, discohesive cells (often signet ring cells) infiltrating the gastric wall diffusely, often causing linitis plastica. Less associated with environmental factors and the Correa cascade. More common in younger patients and associated with germline mutations in CDH1 (E-cadherin), leading to loss of cell-cell adhesion. Other mutations can involve TP53, PIK3CA. Prognosis is generally worse than intestinal-type.
- **E-cadherin (CDH1):** A transmembrane glycoprotein crucial for calcium-dependent cell-cell adhesion in epithelial tissues. Loss of E-cadherin function disrupts adherens junctions, leading to loss of cell cohesion, increased cell motility, and invasion. Germline mutations in CDH1 are strongly associated with hereditary diffuse gastric cancer (HDGC).
- **Linitis Plastica:** A specific pattern of diffuse gastric cancer where the tumor infiltrates the submucosa and muscularis propria, causing thickening, rigidity, and loss of distensibility of the stomach wall. The term refers to the "leather bottle" appearance.
- **Signet Ring Cells:** Malignant cells containing large amounts of intracytoplasmic mucin, which pushes the nucleus to the periphery, resembling a signet ring. These cells are often poorly cohesive and are characteristic of diffuse-type adenocarcinoma.

##### 3. Step-by-Step Clinical Deduction (临床逻辑逐步推理)
1. The patient's presentation (early satiety, weight loss) and imaging (linitis plastica) strongly suggest diffuse-type gastric cancer.
2. The biopsy findings (diffuse infiltration, signet ring cells) confirm diffuse-type histology.
3. The CDH1 mutation confirms the molecular basis often associated with diffuse-type cancer.
4. The question asks to differentiate diffuse-type from intestinal-type.
5. Evaluate option (A): Accurately describes the histology (discohesive signet ring cells, linitis plastica), molecular basis (CDH1/E-cadherin loss), and associations (unrelated to H. pylori/metaplasia) of diffuse-type cancer. It also correctly contrasts this with intestinal-type (cohesive glands, H. pylori link, exophytic/ulcerated mass). This aligns perfectly with the clinical vignette and known pathophysiology.
6. Evaluate option (B): Diffuse-type cancer does not arise exclusively from antral G cells. G cells produce gastrin and are primarily located in the antrum, but diffuse cancer involves epithelial cells throughout the stomach.
7. Evaluate option (C): Intestinal-type cancer is not caused by mutations in the dystrophin gene. Dystrophin mutations cause Duchenne/Becker muscular dystrophy.
8. Evaluate option (D): Diffuse-type cancer has a poor prognosis and does not have a 100% cure rate with antacids. Antacids treat symptoms of acid reflux but do not cure cancer.
9. Evaluate option (E): Intestinal-type cancer is more common in older individuals and is associated with chronic inflammation and metaplasia, which take time to develop. While diffuse-type can occur in younger patients, intestinal-type is not exclusive to children.

##### 4. Distractor Analysis (干扰项逐一深度剖析与鉴别)
- **(A) [Analysis of why Option A is correct]:** This option accurately contrasts the key features of diffuse-type (discohesive signet ring cells, linitis plastica, CDH1 loss, less H. pylori association) with intestinal-type (cohesive glands, H. pylori association, exophytic/ulcerated mass). This aligns with the patient's presentation and molecular findings.
- **(B) [Analysis of why Option B is incorrect]:** Diffuse-type gastric cancer arises from gastric epithelial cells, not specifically antral G cells. G cells are involved in gastrin production, and while they can be involved in gastric neuroendocrine tumors (carcinoids), diffuse gastric adenocarcinoma involves the main gastric epithelium.
- **(C) [Analysis of why Option C is incorrect]:** Mutations in the dystrophin gene are associated with muscular dystrophies, not gastric cancer. Gastric cancer involves mutations in genes like TP53, APC, KRAS, CDH1, etc.
- **(D) [Analysis of why Option D is incorrect]:** Diffuse-type gastric cancer generally has a poorer prognosis than intestinal-type. Oral antacids have no curative effect on gastric cancer.
- **(E) [Analysis of why Option E is incorrect]:** Intestinal-type gastric cancer is more strongly associated with aging and chronic environmental exposures (H. pylori, smoking), making it more common in older adults. While diffuse-type can occur in younger individuals, intestinal-type is not exclusive to children.

##### 5. High-Yield Summary Table (高分必备核心对比表)

| Feature             | Intestinal-Type Gastric Adenocarcinoma | Diffuse-Type Gastric Adenocarcinoma |
|----------------------|----------------------------------------|--------------------------------------|
| **Histology**        | Cohesive glands, papillary structures | Discohesive cells, signet ring cells |
| **Growth Pattern**   | Exophytic, ulcerated mass              | Diffuse infiltration, linitis plastica |
| **Molecular Basis**  | TP53, APC, KRAS, MSI                  | CDH1 (E-cadherin) loss, TP53, PIK3CA |
| **Risk Factors**     | H. pylori, smoking, diet, atrophy, metaplasia | CDH1 mutation, younger age, less environmental link |
| **Prognosis**        | Generally better                       | Generally worse                      |
| **Location**         | Often antrum/pylorus                   | Can involve any part of stomach      |

#### Educational Objective / 核心考点总结:
Understanding the distinct histological, molecular, clinical, and epidemiological features of intestinal-type versus diffuse-type gastric adenocarcinoma is crucial for diagnosis, prognosis, and potential targeted therapies. Diffuse-type is characterized by loss of cell adhesion (CDH1 mutation), leading to discohesive cells (like signet ring cells) and diffuse infiltration (linitis plastica), while intestinal-type follows the Correa cascade, is linked to H. pylori, and forms cohesive glandular structures.
